*5.4 Area loading design (18 Nov 2026 — "areas, short load")*
- **An area** is a piece of a city built exactly like today's single world, from its own files: a base line (`routes/cities/<city>-<area>.gpx`: one open line, 10 m samples), a city config (`tools/city/<city>-<area>.json` → city data `routes/scenes/<city>-<area>-city.json`), a terrain grid (`…-terrain.grid.bin.gz`), a scene (`routes/scenes/<city>-<area>.json`, its metre keys on its own line, its own `qcAccept`) and a network fragment (`tools/city/<city>-<area>.network.json`: its junctions, segments and presets). Each area has a catalog entry `<city>-<area>` with `"area": "<city>"`: not in the route list, but the QC tools, snapshots and the preview open it like any route. An agent building an area touches only its own files, plus one line in the city's network spec (`areas`) and presets that cross into it.
- **One frame per city.** The city spec sets `origin` (lat/lon): every area of the city is projected around it, so the same street is at the same x/z whichever area draws it. City data and terrain grids are built on a city lattice (`lattice` in the city config: an anchor and the cell sizes; grids snap to it), so where two areas overlap their heights are the same samples.
- **The network spans areas** (`bikefree-network-2`): each segment (edge) belongs to one area and is an interval of that area's line; a junction where segments of two areas meet is a **portal**, found on both lines (`passes` per area). An area's line also runs an **apron** along every other area's segment at its portals (≥ 400 m, the whole segment if shorter): drawn as route — road, kerbs, street kit, junction curves — but not a segment of this area (`routeAprons` in the city data; `aprons` in the network: which segment, which way, from where). Ghost stretches stay the way a line repeats itself; areas shorten them (no line has to go back through another area).
- **Which area is drawn:** one at a time. The area drawn keeps drawing while it covers the rider's place and the next 200 m of the path (its own segments or its aprons); when it doesn't, the area that covers the rider and reaches furthest ahead takes over — so the swap happens inside an apron, 200 m beyond the portal, with the junction itself seen whole on either side of it, and where both areas show the same streets. Poses are taken on the drawn area's line (the path maps onto aprons too: `worldAt(p, area)`), so the rider always rides the road that is drawn; a turn between segments of two areas is a curve like any other (straight on is seamless where the drawn line runs straight on).
- **Residency:** the area drawn is always loaded; an area the path (and the pacer's) enters within 1.5 km ahead is loaded and built hidden (scenery data, its ground and city chunks streamed around the camera at a small frame budget, life not ticked), so it is ready at the swap; an area not drawn, not within 1.5 km ahead and not within 600 m behind is released (disposed). If the swap comes before the area is ready: a short fade to the sky colour, wait, swap, fade back — the ride carries on underneath. All areas' GPX lines load with the network (small): path routes, minimap, planner, thumbnails and recording need them; the heavy scenery (city data, terrain grid, scene) only when the world needs it.
- **Shared in the world:** renderer, sky, lights, post and look, weather (one set of scenery uniforms for all areas), rider, pacer, course arches (placed on the drawn area's line, else their own). Per area: scenery (StylizedTerrain: ground, city, buildings, life), road and shoulder ribbons, route index, junction plan. Baked light bakes the drawn area; after a swap the ride re-bake (time-sliced, the old bake in use until it's done) takes over — around the swap both areas are the same streets, so the old bake fits there.
- **Unchanged for the rider:** the path route (samples copied from each segment's own line), prompt, lock, curves, speed cap, laps, profile, pacer (`PathFollower`), presets, free ride, planner, recording — all network-level, area-agnostic. Minimap, planner and thumbnails draw each segment from its own line. Life: each area's people and traffic run while it is drawn; nothing is carried across a swap (it happens 200 m past a junction, where the life of both is placed from the same streets).
- **Catalog and saved routes:** the city keeps one catalog entry (`pittsburgh`) with its presets; its `file`/`scene` are the first area's (the scenery choice and previews). Segment keys ("E>I") are city-wide, so presets, free ride and saved routes (`settings.netRoutes`, `keysToIds`) carry on.
- **QC per area:** `qc_course --strict <city>-<area>` and `qc_clipping <city>-<area>` on each area's line; `qc_network` rides every way through every junction, portals included (poses on the drawn area at each step, crossing the swap); `build_network --check`; unit tests ride across each portal (no jump, road under the rider in the drawn area, prompt and minimap continuous); `snap --path` across a portal.
- **Performance:** one area drawn → draw calls and triangles at a boundary are one area's (≤ today's whole tour; target ≤ +25 %); memory: two areas' scenery resident near a portal (each smaller than today's tour). Building an area is main-thread work, done ≥ 1.5 km before the portal — in frame slices (F30: 4 ms a frame, 10 ms within 500 m, 40 ms behind the start loader or a swap's fade).
- **Migration (Pittsburgh):** Core — downtown, the North Shore, the South Side, Mount Washington, the Jail Trail, the Hot Metal and West End bridges; West End — Elliott and the overlook (D>O); Oakland & Schenley (I>J, J>K, K>J); Allegheny & Troy Hill (A>P, P>N, N>P, N>M, M>B). Portals: I (Core / Oakland), D (Core / West End), A and B (Core / Allegheny). Core: A>B, B>E, E>G, G>H, H>G, G>D, D>A, E>I, I>H. Each area's line is cut from today's tour on its own 10 m samples (`tools/build_city.py` `slice`: the master legs routed as today, then the pieces kept), so every segment keeps its exact length and every preset its lap, and scene metre keys move by an offset. Allegheny & Troy Hill arrived on the tour (5.3/5.6) before the split and became the fourth area the same way.

*Adding an area (the recipe — an agent touches only its own files and two shared lines)*
1. **Plan its line**: one open line (`legs`, as any city config) through the area's own segments, plus an **apron** — a leg with `"apron": true`, ≥ 400 m — along every other area's segment at each of its portals, and `"ghost": true` legs where the line has to go back to a junction. A portal is an existing junction: use its id and name as the neighbour's network fragment has them. The neighbour's files need no change: where the neighbour has no apron on the new area's arm, the swap happens 200 m before the portal, inside the new area's apron.
2. **City config** `tools/city/<city>-<area>.json`: the legs, `"lattice": "<city>"` (heights on the city's grid lattice), the city's settings (water, radii, styles); `"osmCache": "<city>"` while its OSM box covers the area (a new box: its own cache). Build: `python3 tools/build_city.py <city>-<area>` → `routes/cities/<city>-<area>.gpx` and `routes/scenes/<city>-<area>-city.json` (with `routeAprons`, `routeGhosts`).
3. **Scene** `routes/scenes/<city>-<area>.json`: a sibling's city-wide settings (look, lighting, palette, weather, `origin`, `terrainBuild` with the shared `box` and `lat0`), its own metre-keyed items (road zones, street-kit zones, signs, views, `qcAccept`) on its own line, its `preview`. Then `python3 tools/build_terrain_grid.py <city>-<area>`.
4. **Catalog**: an entry in `routes/catalog.json` — `id`, `name`, `park`, `file`, `scene`, `expectedKm`, `eleSmoothM` as the city's, `"area": "<city>"` (not listed; QC, snapshots and the preview open it).
5. **Network**: `tools/city/<city>-<area>.network.json` (`nodes` it passes — portals included —, `edges` it owns with names both ways, presets inside it), one line in `tools/city/<city>-network.spec.json` `areas`, and presets that cross areas there. `node tools/build_network.mjs` (checks a portal is the same place and height on both lines, matches every apron to its segment), then `npm run routes:build`.
6. **QC**: `npm test`, `npm run qc:network` (every way through every junction; every preset across the areas: no jump at a swap, never a ghost under the rider), `node tools/qc_course.mjs --strict <city>-<area>`, `node tools/qc_clipping.mjs <city>-<area>`, snapshots either side of each portal and of the same spot in both areas: `npm run snap -- --path <ids> --area <a> <city>@<m>`.
7. **Aprons — one mechanism (F60)**: every apron leg, and the ghost back between a portal's two aprons, carries `"samplesOf": {"gpx": <the neighbour's line>, "fromM", "toM"}` (the neighbour's app metres, on its 10 m samples; `toM < fromM` runs it backward). `build_city.py` routes the leg like any other (its streets are this area's too), then splices in the neighbour's own points (`splice_samples`): the apron matches its segment sample for sample, the portal is the same place and height on both lines, and the copy's route kinds and bridges are the neighbour's (the same paint and decks either side of a swap). A routed stretch that leads into a copy must hold a ghost (a hidden zig there keeps the 10 m phase); a stretch after a copy starts on the copy's last sample (eased over 60 m). Options: `"portals"` on a copy (junctions on it that the line comes back to from another street: the line's end meets the copy's sample there and later passes are levelled just under it), `"zigM"` on a ghost's copy (keeps the 10 m phase of what follows — used to migrate an area without moving its samples); hill junctions passed more than once: `heightPins` with `"everyPass": true` (each pass's nearest sample), `"everyPass": "round"` (all within 8 m) or `"level": "first"` (the others take the first pass's plane) — `junction_heights`. Order the legs so the line passes each junction on through rather than turning round at it; an OSM box reaching past the shared cache's first box gives `osmOrigin` (the cache's lattice corner: tiles, not aprons). (Before F60 the GAP area aligned a routed apron with `trimStartM` and the North Side copied its first leg with `copy`; both now use `samplesOf`.)
(Pittsburgh's four were cut from the one tour instead: `"slice"` in their city configs and `tools/slice_scene.mjs` for their scenes — the migration's way of keeping every metre; new areas use legs.)

Its grade, its place in the world and its minimap dot follow its own way; test in `test/network.test.mjs`

- [x] F30 Building an area is main-thread work, seconds long: here (headless Chrome, Linux) Oakland's landscape 2.5 s and city 5.6 s, the core's landscape 5.3 s (Node). A planned ride builds its areas at the start, behind the loader, and keeps them, so it never stalls; off the plan (a free ride, a turn into an area the plan doesn't visit) the area is built 1.5 km ahead and the view stalls for that long. Most of it is the street processing chain (road-meets: resolveOverlaps, followMovedNodes, joinCrossings, planJunctions — ~60 % in a profile): bake its result into the city data at build time (it is deterministic from the data), or build in a worker / in frame-sized slices → (18 Nov, sprint-f30) built in frame slices: every step of an area's build is a job (js/world/toolbox/core/jobs.js: generators that pause at `due()` when their slice is used up; `drain` runs one at once, `runSlice(job, ms)` a slice) — the landscape and the whole street chain (cityStreets, alignStreetProfiles, the road-meets passes, planJunctions, junctionTops, the riverfront plan, the rivers' fog field), the scenery's ground/water/roadside, the road and shoulder, the kit's assets (awaited), then the city (buildings, water and river walls, street kit, building kit plan, streets/junction meshes/lamps, bridges, trees, life) and later each streamed city chunk (`_stream`: a chunk may pause between buildings; its street-kit work and the building kit's homes too). The world runs the builds a slice a frame (`_runAreaJobs`): 4 ms ahead, 10 ms once the path enters the area within 500 m, 40 ms for the plan's areas at the start (behind the loader) or the one a swap waits for (behind the fade, kept). Same code, same order: the result is the same — `tools/area_build_check.mjs` builds each of the 8 areas sliced (4 ms) and the old way (the pre-F30 code) and fingerprints every object (geometry, transforms, instances, materials): identical for all 8; scene snapshots (--fingerprint, 8 spots) pixel-identical, objects identical but the buses and the Point fountain (life). Not baked into the data (no data change: the junction plan's surfaces are closures; F92) and no worker (three.js objects in most steps). Measured (`tools/ride_area_load.mjs`: free ride e2+ from 2.0 to 4.5 km at 10 m/s into Oakland, headless Chrome, the 2-core Linux box under load from other runs, no draw): before, the build stalled two frames of 3.7 s and 9.4 s; after, the build is 9.2 s of main-thread work in 1,229 slices (longest 247 ms, p99 frame work 16 ms), ready 25 s (250 m) after it starts, 1.45 km before the swap; no fade. The longest slices left are GC pauses and single big items (F90). Heap 1.27–1.29 GB peak both ways.

### Minimap zooms in and follows you *(branch `sprint-minimap`, from `pittsburgh-network-test`, 18 Nov 2026)*
The user (18 Nov): "Minimap should zoom in and not show the entirety of the map. Moves as you move. Arrow should be 50% smaller." Decided:
- [x] **Zoomed and following:** about 3 km across the minimap's larger side, centred a little ahead of the rider (the view leans halfway to the point 900 m on along the route, at most 450 m; the lean eases over ~0.8 s, so a turn chosen at a junction or the path refilling never jumps it); it moves as you ride (redrawn 5× a second, under a pixel each time). Near a route's ends it keeps following (empty space beyond); a loop wraps through its start. `js/minimap-view.js` (the view maths), `js/views.js` drawMinimap / drawNetworkMinimap
- [x] **North up:** the map doesn't rotate; the arrow turns with the heading
- [x] **Every route:** park routes, network rides (network faint, path bold, the next options), free ride, scenery rides, demo. Settings → Minimap: Zoomed in (now the default; a saved "Whole course", the old default, becomes Zoomed in once) · Whole course · Off; <kbd>M</kbd> cycles them. No tap on the minimap (there wasn't one)
- [x] **Arrow 50 % smaller** (same shape and outline, scaled by half)
- [x] **Cheap per draw:** every line is projected to metres once (per route, per network, per path version) and cut into 32-point chunks with bounding boxes; a draw walks only the chunks in view, one Path2D per style. Headless (swiftshader, 1440×900, JS time of the draw calls, the 3D loop stopped), at the same 3 km zoom: Pittsburgh network 0.84 → 0.14 ms (whole course 0.38 → 0.16 ms), Yosemite 0.10 → 0.08 ms, the free ride's loop 0.09 → 0.10 ms; with the raster flushed (a 1 px read back) 4.1 → 3.1 ms on the network, ~1 ms on a park route. Crisp at devicePixelRatio 2 (the canvas is sized ×dpr as before)
- QC: `test/minimap.test.mjs` (3 km span, centring and lean, north up, clipping runs, the eased lean, no jump through a loop's start, a network clipped to the view, half-size arrow, dpr 2); `node tools/snap_minimap.mjs [--bench]` (the ride HUD on a Pittsburgh junction, Yosemite and a free ride at 1440×900 and 390×844 → `tools/snapshots/minimap/`, not committed)

### Smooth sharp turns *(test branch `smooth-turns-test`, from `main` after PR #34)*
**Goal:** roads and their paint did not look smooth through sharp turns on Pittsburgh and Alpe d'Huez: the road was faceted, the lines kinked and the rider and camera swung round the corner. Fix it for those two routes only, at the root, without changing ride distance or the profile.

**Causes**
- The route is kept as samples exactly 10 m apart. The Alpe's hairpins put up to 133° into one sample (Pittsburgh's corners up to 53°), so the road drawn through them (straight 10 m pieces on the Alpe; a Catmull-Rom curve through the samples on Pittsburgh) turned on a 0.5 m (Alpe) / 5 m (Pittsburgh) radius, with 85° / 19° between consecutive cross-sections.
- The paint is drawn in road coordinates on the ribbon, so it kinked wherever the ribbon did.
- The rider's heading is the chord over ±6 m of the same line, so on the Alpe it turned up to 22°/m and jumped by 24°/m per metre at the hairpins.

**Passes**
- [x] `roadAlign.turns` (`js/world/road-align.js`, run by `makeRouteIndex`): every stretch tighter than `minRadiusM` (8 m) is redrawn with its heading eased (curvature built up over a few metres, total turn and end headings kept), the end offset taken out smoothly, and the line spaced evenly; less radius where the road would move more than `maxShiftM` (6 m). The 10 m samples inside move onto it, so the ground, rails and walls follow the same road
- [x] The drawn road (`denseRoad`): a cross-section every 3° where a 10 m piece would step more than 6°; the curve's own tangents for the cross-sections; ribbon edges clamped so they never run backwards (`ribbonSections`). Everything else keeps its old cut
- [x] Gated: only Pittsburgh and Alpe d'Huez have `turns`; the city preset leaves it out; the refactor guard shows the other routes unchanged
- [x] `test/smooth-turns.test.mjs` (fails on main): cross-section steps, folds, tightest radius, rider turn rate and its change, ride distance / elevation / drawn length, rider on the drawn road
- [ ] Ride the Alpe's hairpins and Pittsburgh's corners on the Mac and the Air (githack link)

### Pittsburgh phase 6: clean roads, smooth swaps, polish *(test branch `pittsburgh-phase6-test`, from `main` after PR #38, 19 Nov 2026; one PR at the end)*
Decided with the user (19 Nov; project doc `claude/pittsburgh-phase5-plan.md`):
- [ ] 6.1 **No road clipping**, all three kinds, every area: ground over streets and sidewalks; vehicles through poles, walls and buildings; anything cutting the ridden road (gaps, steps, z-fighting). Drive the clipping warnings toward zero, not only 0 FAIL (F11, F49, F87, F167, F178, F180)
  - **Done (branch `sprint-clip`, 19 Nov; not ticked: 2,755 → 1,207 warnings, 0 FAIL on every area, but not zero)**. Fixes at the source, no QC limits raised, no `qcAccept` added:
  - Vehicles (`js/world/toolbox/city/life.js`): drive ways are cut (`_openJob`) where a car would be inside a building drawn down to the street (OSM service ways through passages and under blocks; walls within 0.5 m of the lane, for the street kit's facades) or under a deck's slab with less than a bus's height (3.6 m) under it — cars stop and turn round there; light-rail and rail tracks lie at a street's own height where they run in or across its carriageway (extra points only near streets, so the triangles fell); incline cabins stop short of the route's road (a Mon Incline cabin crossed Grandview Ave: a vehicle-through-rider FAIL on the merged 6.2 code)
  - Bridges (`bridges.js`): no deck railing across another carriageway at about the deck's level (ramps merging, the other half of a divided road); lamp posts (`city.js` `_underDeck`) not under another deck's slab
  - Street kit (`street-kit.js`): no furniture standing in another street's carriageway (the route's own street keeps its curbs); kit street trees 2.3 m clear of every wall's facade
  - Ground (`landscape.js` heightAt): never over a street's carriageway, sidewalk or path more than 9 m from the route (beyond the route's bank: within 9 m the 6 m mesh can't step, F201)
  - People (F180): trail walkers start on the land side and turn back where their side of the trail is the river; strollers at hotspots (the Point) turn away from the water
  - Birds: the gull flock is named (`Birds · gulls`): the clipping QC took the unnamed instanced mesh for lamp posts "floating" 40–60 m over the rivers (all 10 core `floating` warnings)
  - QC tool: `qc_clipping --why` (every vehicle-solid hit with its car, way, street, and where the cut triangles are relative to it) and `--life-only` (the life sim alone, ~3 min an area)
  - Tried and left out: deck heights in the map data (`build_city.py`: chain decks across OSM layer changes, where they dipped to the DEM ground; lift decks to clear the streets they cross). It cleared many decks over streets but caused course findings (street creases, junction-over-road at core 18.07 km and North Side 18.00 km, road-buried at core 6.21 km); with the runtime rules above the vehicle counts were as good without it (core 265 vs 303), so the data is unchanged (F200)
  - Warnings (19 Nov, merged with 6.2 at 08cd816; before = the 19 Nov baseline): core 748 → 373 (vehicle-clips-solid 602 → 262, ground-over-street 92 → 71, floating 10 → 0, person-in-water 2 → 0, posts 16 → 13, trees 18 → 19, boats 3, z-fight 2, train 1, buildings 1, structure 1); Allegheny 390 → 202 (vehicles 148, trees 28, ground 12, posts 8, boats 2, trains 2, structure 1, z-fight 1); West End 194 → 44 (vehicles 20, ground 18, trees 4, post 1, z-fight 1); Oakland 114 → 115 (vehicles 57, ground 37, posts 17, trees 4); Polish Hill 76 → 47 (vehicles 35, trees 7, posts 4, z-fight 1); GAP & Homestead 211 → 44 (vehicles 17, posts 8, ground 7, trees 6, buildings 2, structures 2, z-fights 2); North Side 371 → 142 (vehicles 99, trees 17, ground 13, posts 6, z-fights 5, boats 2); East End 74 → 34 (vehicles 19, ground 14, tree 1); Slopes 577 → 206 (vehicles 126, ground 51, trees 14, posts 4, z-fights 4, structures 3, trains 3, buildings 1). Total 2,755 → 1,207. (Static checks from the full run, the life sim from a `--life-only` run on the final code)
  - QC: `npm test` 262 of 262; `qc:network` all clean; `qc_course --strict` 0 fail / 0 warn on every route and area; clipping 0 FAIL on all nine areas (the subway area not run: not this item's data). Performance: no new meshes or draws; core 4.16 → 4.13–4.15 M triangles in the QC scene (fewer lamps, furniture and rail points)
  - Follow-ups (F200–F219 kept for this item):
  - [ ] F200 Deck heights at OSM layer changes: a deck chained only to its own layer is anchored to the DEM ground where it meets a deck of another layer (Fort Pitt / Parkway / North Shore ramps 1–3 m over the streets under them; some joins 20 m low). The tried fix (patch kept out of git: chain through any shared node, ground ends only where no deck or a ground street meets; `lift_over_route` over crossed streets with `end_grade` .08) needs the course findings above solved first (street creases where ground streets follow moved decks; junctions over the road)
  - [ ] F201 Ground over streets within 9 m of the route (most of what's left: core 71, Slopes 51, Oakland 37): the 6 m ground mesh can't step from the route's bank down to a lower street beside it. Automatic fine ground cells (3 m, `groundMesh.fineCells`) where a street lies 6–14 m from the route at another height; cost ~1.5 k triangles per 96 m patch
  - [ ] F202 Vehicles still through "Bridges" (adjacent decks and the route's deck beside streets: core street 2588's deck, ramps sharing a slab with a ground street) and through in-street light-rail tracks (the Allentown Line on Arlington Ave, core 12.1–12.7 km: hits remain though the rails now lie at the street's height — look with `--why`)
  - [ ] F203 Vehicles through the subway headhouses (core 2.46 km, a one-way street through the Gateway / Wood St headhouse and its fittings): the subway kit's (subway.js) placement, for its owner

  - **Life across a swap (F31):** `js/world/toolbox/city/life-handoff.js`. At a swap the new area's life (`CityLife.takeOver`, run on its first tick) takes over the old one's agents near the camera (people 330 m, cyclists 380 m, cars and buses 480 m — their respawn radii): each keeps its look, model, paint, pace and drawn place and goes onto the same street of the new area's networks (the old way's centre line at its distance matched to the nearest new way running the same way at the same level: ≤ 2.5 m and ≤ 37°, then ≤ 4.5 m and ≤ 57°; a one-way only the way it may be driven unless it is the very street; its side kept in the world). People standing about a square keep their spot; oncoming traffic on the route's street goes onto the new line where it is (the swap is inside an apron: the same street); trains onto the same line where it carries the whole train; boats on the same rivers. What can't be matched (a street only one area keeps) is left dead and appears again out of sight, and nothing new appears in view (the life counts as under way). Car paint is now the car's own: it followed the instance slot, so a car changed colour in view when one before it in the list went (and couldn't have been carried). life.js changes: the `takeOver` hook in `tick`, the paint (two lines).

The swap (`useArea`) puts the new area's own bake in the uniforms at once; with none yet (a swap sooner than the bake), the old one stays until the new area's is done, as before.   - [x] F220 **Bug (user, on main): choosing another way at a junction sometimes gave a beige screen until the choice was undone.** Cause: the beige is the area fade (`World._fade`, the fog colour). Fix: (1) `NetRide.areasReachable` — every arm of each junction within 800 m whose choice isn't locked, and the way on from it within that reach (the plan, a free ride's hint, else straight on), with every area whose line covers it (an apron counts: `areaFor` may pick it) — is built hidden as well, and let go once the junction is passed; (2) while the wanted area isn't built the world keeps drawing the current one as long as it covers the rider (its apron, `areaChoice`), and fades only once the rider is off its streets — past 1.5 s under the fade the build gets 250 ms a frame behind a "Loading the streets ahead…" notice; (3) a failed load or build is held back (10 s × tries, given up after 3) and the drawn area stays — no fade. Test (test/area-swaps.test.mjs): for every junction and every arm in Pittsburgh (choose at the prompt, ride on), each area the world then wants is in its need set 700 m before (32 swaps after a choice; the shortest lead 340 m, from the start of the way in); the stay / swap / fade choice; the failed load not retried every frame. - [x] 6.11 **Hill feel, up and down** (asked 19 Nov; done, branch `sprint-hillfeel`, below). The user rides ERG (the default). Compared with Zwift, a hill's start is a sudden jump in difficulty and a descent drops the watts you can make at once. Review (19 Nov, js/physics.js, js/session.js `_control`):
  - Cause 1, no real momentum: ERG's target (`ergTargetForGear`) is the power to hold the speed your *cadence and gear* imply on the grade, not the simulated bike's speed. Momentum is only a fixed 3 s ease of the grade (`MOMENTUM_TAU_S`), so a 6 % hill reaches ~95 % of its full wattage in ~9 s whatever your speed, and the jump starts the instant the grade changes
  - Cause 2, descents: on a negative grade the power for the cadence speed goes to ~0 (the aero force is taken at the cadence speed, e.g. 27 km/h, not the faster descent speed) and the target clamps to 0 W: nothing to push against
  - Grade itself is already smoothed over ±30 m (js/gpx.js); city routes are limited further by build_city
  - Do (decided): **real momentum** — the ERG target from the simulated bike (its speed, mass and acceleration: `stepSpeed`), blended with the gear/cadence demand, so a hill bites gradually as the bike slows (**~10 s to full climbing resistance on a 5–8 % hill at ~25 km/h**, longer at higher speed, shorter at low speed); **downhill pedal feel** — pedalling on a descent meets resistance from the descent speed's aero and the bike's inertia (a floor that fades as you spin out in the gear), never 0 W while you pedal; **gradual grade changes** — the felt grade blends in over distance (longer at speed) and changes at most ~1 %/s. SIM mode gets the same felt-grade shaping. Hill feel (25–100 %) still scales it. Not changing: SIM stays opt-in (ERG default)
  - QC: unit tests of the ERG target through a hill's start, a crest and a descent (time to 90 %, no step over N W per second, a descent floor); `npm test`; a ride trace (tools) on Alpe d'Huez and a Pittsburgh Dirty Dozen climb. The user checks it on the bike against Zwift. Follow-ups F300–F309
  - **Built** (branch `sprint-hillfeel`, from `pittsburgh-phase6-test` at 08cd816; js/physics.js, js/session.js `_control`). Three stages from the road to the trainer, each tick:
    1. **Felt grade, shaped** (`shapeGrade`, both modes): Hill feel × road grade, blended in over a distance of 2 m + 1 s × speed (longer at speed; as if rolling at 1.5 m/s when stopped, so free ride ▲ ▼ still arrives), then **at most 1 %/s** (`GRADE_RATE_PCT_S`). SIM sends this grade (its trainer's own flywheel is the momentum); `session.shapedGrade`
    2. **Momentum** (`momentumGrade`, ERG): the hill's force first slows the bike, and the pedals take up the load in proportion to the share of its speed the bike has given up — the hill bites over τ = speed / `MOMENTUM_ACCEL` (1.3 m/s²): ~5 s at 25 km/h, ~7.5 s at 35 km/h, shorter as the bike slows on the climb (the on-screen bike's speed from `stepSpeed`, so it follows the simulated bike); also ≤ 1 %/s. The same at a crest (the load eases as the bike picks up speed) and into a descent; `session.pedalGrade` (shown as "Trainer feels" in Settings' diagnostics in ERG)
    3. **ERG target** (`ergTargetForBike`): the gear/cadence demand (power for your gear's speed) on the momentum grade — so a **shift acts at once** (the gear's speed is in the demand, not in the lag) — never under the **descent floor** while you pedal (`descentFloorW`): 70 % of the air + rolling drag at the bike's speed (up to the fastest your gear still drives it), taken at your gear's speed, fading as the bike runs away from your gear (gear speed / bike speed 0.95 → 0.5) to 40 % of that, ≥ 10 W; coasting (< 15 rpm) is 0
    - Unchanged: workouts' ERG targets, escalators (`carry`, ERG 0 / `CARRY_SIM_GRADE`), the on-screen speed (`stepSpeed` on the real grade with the power you make: in ERG the target, so speed and trainer agree), ERG default / SIM opt-in, Hill feel 25–100 %. `ergTargetForGear` stays (the plain gear demand); `easeGrade` / `MOMENTUM_TAU_S` (the fixed 3 s ease) are gone
  - **Numbers** — a step onto a grade in a free ride, rider at 85 rpm in the gear that makes the speed, Hill feel 50 %, the session's target watts (before = 08cd816):

    | | before: at 2 s / 5 s / 10 s, t50, t90, max step | after |
    |---|---|---|
    | 6 % at 15 km/h (gear 7), 29 → 142 W | 84 / 121 / 138 W, 2.3 s, 7.0 s, 30 W/s | 55 / 114 / 139 W, 3.5 s, **7.0 s**, 23 W/s |
    | 5 % at 25 km/h (gear 12), 91 → 248 W | 167 / 218 / 242, 2.3, 7.3, 41 | 116 / 177 / 230, 4.8, **10.8**, 22 |
    | 6 % at 25 km/h, 91 → 279 W | 183 / 243 / 272, 2.3, 7.0, 49 | 117 / 193 / 260, 4.8, **10.3**, 27 |
    | 8 % at 25 km/h, 91 → 341 W | 213 / 294 / 332, 2.3, 7.0, 65 | 118 / 225 / 320, 4.8, **9.5**, 38 |
    | 6 % at 35 km/h (gear 16), 217 → 479 W | 345 / 430 / 470, 2.3, 7.0, 68 | 243 / 325 / 419, 6.0, **14.3**, 28 |
    | −6 % at 25 km/h, from 91 W | 0 W after 1.8 s | 70 / 50 / 35 W, settles at 28 W (spun out in gear 12) |
    | crest 6 % → 0 at ~16 km/h | — | half off in ~4 s, 90 % in ~14 s (the tail as the bike gets back to its gear's speed) |
  - **Ride traces** (`node tools/ride_feel_trace.mjs [--trace <alpe|canton|descent|cantonDown>] [--gear N] [--csv f]`: the real Session in Node, a trainer holding the ERG target with a 1 s response, 85 rpm in one gear, Hill feel 50 %, before vs after):
    - Alpe d'Huez, Bourg d'Oisans into the first hairpins (112–117 km, gear 12): the GPX's grade already rises slowly here (±60 m smoothing, 0 → 12 % over ~60 s), so before and after are close: max step 9 W/s both, the climb's onset ~20 W softer (at 255 s: 324 → 300 W), the same 506 W on the 12–13 % ramps
    - Canton Ave climbed (the Slopes route 8.95 → 8.15 km reversed: down the street before it at 42 km/h, then the 13 % smoothed wall; gear 12): before 0 W on the way down, then 0 → 156 → 349 W at 14/16/20 s; after 35 W on the way down, 42 → 58 → 216 W, and 90 % of the wall's 534 W ~2 s later (the bike loses its speed fast on 13 %); max step 59 → 55 W/s (it's the 1 %/s limit at gear 12's speed); never under 35 W
    - Alpe d'Huez's descent (107.4–111 km): gear 12 before 0 W for most of it (min 0, steps of −38 W/s), after 30 W at least, steps ≤ 15 W/s, the felt grade ≤ 0.29 %/s (was 0.65); gear 20 before 0–670 W (±70 W/s), after 141–626 W (+15/−34 W/s)
    - Down Canton Ave (gear 12): before 0 W from 32 s, then **258 W at once** in the dip at the bottom (+7 % at 47 km/h; +81 W/s, felt 1.55 %/s); after 57 → 30 W down the hill and **33 W** in the dip (the bike's still at 45 km/h: momentum carries it up), ≤ 5 W/s, felt ≤ 0.65 %/s
  - **What to feel on the bike** (vs Zwift): a hill's start comes on over ~10 s at 25 km/h (the first 2 s barely change), faster when you're slow, slower when you come in fast; a short kicker after a descent is mostly carried by your speed; a shift still bites at once; on descents the watts fade over ~10 s and stay at ~30 W in a flat-road gear — shift up for more (gear 20 keeps ~140–200 W); stop pedalling and it's 0
  - QC (19 Nov): `test/hill-feel.test.mjs` (new, 9 tests through the real Session: t90 at 25 km/h 8.5–12 s for 5/6/8 %, 15 < 25 < 35 km/h, ≤ 60 W/s, ≤ 1 %/s in ERG and SIM at Hill feel 50/100, SIM's shaped grade ahead of ERG's, Hill feel scales, crest, descent floor and spin-out fade, coasting 0, a shift's target the same tick, workouts and escalators untouched), `test/ride-feel.test.mjs` (the 3 s ease test replaced by the shaping and momentum ones); `npm test` 272 of 272 (final code)
  - Follow-ups (F300–F309 kept for hill feel):
  - [ ] F300 On the bike against Zwift: the ~10 s hill start, the descent floor (~30 W spun out in gear 12, `DESCENT_FLOOR_SHARE` 0.7 / `DESCENT_FLOOR_MIN` 0.4) and the 1 %/s limit are first guesses; tune `MOMENTUM_ACCEL`, the floor and `GRADE_RATE_PCT_S` from how it feels
  - [ ] F301 Steep walls entered fast (Canton after the descent): the bike sheds speed in a few seconds, so the wall still comes on over ~6 s at up to the 1 %/s limit (55 W/s in gear 12). If it's still a shock, a lower W/s cap on the target, or the limit in %/s scaled down at high gear speeds
  - [ ] F302 SIM gets the shaping but not the momentum (its trainer's flywheel and the rider's cadence carry it); check whether SIM hills now feel slower to arrive than before (no 3 s ease, but the 1 %/s limit)
  - [ ] F303 A crest's relief has a long tail (90 % in ~14 s): τ grows as the bike speeds back up. A crest could use the gear's speed or a faster release if it feels sticky
  - [ ] F304 The momentum uses the on-screen bike's speed: junction speed caps and the escalator's slow-down shorten τ there (the load comes faster); demo mode's fixed speed holds τ fixed
  - [ ] F305 The descent floor uses the drag at the bike's speed, not the grade: a gentle descent in a gear that keeps up feels like ~70 % of the flat; Hill feel doesn't scale the floor
  - [ ] F306 Settings' "Trainer feels" diagnostic shows the momentum grade in ERG and the shaped grade in SIM; the HUD's grade is still the road's

**6.8 Route preview images** *(branch `sprint-previews`, from `pittsburgh-phase6-test` at 4e5f004, 19 Nov 2026)* — built; wrapped up with a partial render (F270)
- **Menu:** the route preview pane (index.html `#pvShots`, under the facts) shows a route's 2–3 pictures in riding order, each with a caption ("Start · North Shore Water Steps", "Highlight · Bixby Creek Bridge", "Climb · Troy Hill & Rialto Street, 25 %"): a row of three on desktop, a swipeable strip (64 % wide cards, scroll-snap) under 760 px; light and dark from the theme tokens; `loading="lazy"`; a tap enlarges (`#shotDlg`: ‹ › and arrow keys, Escape or the backdrop closes). A route with no pictures shows nothing (the strip is hidden). Kept while the start is dragged (no reload). `js/route-previews.js` (manifest → shots, HTML, cache pruning), wired in js/app.js `renderShots`
- **Pictures:** `routes/previews/<world>-<metre>[-<subject>].webp`, 480×270 WebP (q 0.72, drawn at 960×540 and scaled down), 8–16 kB each (budget 45 kB each, 8 MB in all; a full set is ~103 pictures ≈ 1.3 MB). Shared: a spot several presets ride is one file (58 routes → 103 pictures). Daylight as the app shows it (midday; Pittsburgh's baked golden light), clear weather
- **Spots** (`tools/preview_spots.mjs`, pure Node): every listed park route (11, deprecated ones included), every Pittsburgh preset (46) and the free ride, each on its own track (a park's outbound leg; a preset's first lap through its areas, rendered on each area's own world at the world metre, so the riding direction is kept). Start (named after the start junction, a named place within 400 m, else the route), the highlight (best score), and a third: the steepest climb when steep (city 150 m ≥ 7 %, park 500 m ≥ 5 %; escalators and tunnels skipped), else a second highlight of another kind, else a gentler climb. Candidates: scene `views` (their own postcard camera), styled / named bridges (city `routeBridges`, park `bridges` / `bridgeRanges`), Grandview's `overlooks`, hero towers ahead, park formations and lakes ahead, Tour cols and named crowd spots, stops, signs, towns, the author's preview spot; nothing underground but a view. Hand picks: `routes/previews/picks.json` (per route: start / highlight / third with `atM`, `name`, `of: <candidate name>`, `view`, `climb`, `off`; used for Big Sur → Bixby Creek Bridge, Route 28, the subway's North Side station)
- **Subject check** (after the coordinator's review: chase-camera highlights didn't show their subject): a highlight with a subject (bridge, tower, landform, overlook view) is rendered from a postcard camera over the road (bridge: from its approach, 0.9 × its length back, 10 m behind and up; tower 6/6 m; landform 20 m up; overlook 3 m) pointed at the subject (no more than ~10° up), and checked before it is kept: in frame, filling enough of it (bridge 22 %, tower 16 %, landform 8 %, overlook 25 %) and seen (rays to its points against the terrain's height function and every drawn mesh but the rider, sky, clouds and see-through things). A failed check is recorded (`renders.json` `checks`, per world hash) and the picker takes the next spot on the next run — e.g. Monument Valley's East Mitten (hidden by the ground); Acadia's Jordan Pond and Bixby Creek Bridge now fill the picture
- **Tool:** `node tools/build_previews.mjs` (plan, render, encode, manifests) · `--stale` (missing / stale only) · `--only <route>` · `--park <world>` · `--plan` (re-pick, rewrite manifests, no render) · `--check` (missing / stale pictures, changed picks, unused files, budget; exit 1) · `--limit <n>` worlds per run (batches under `flock /tmp/heavy.lock`; loop until "rendering 0"). One page per world, seeking along it. Stale = the world's data files, the spot or `RENDER` (camera, size, version) changed; code changes aren't tracked (bump `RENDER.version`). Out: `routes/previews/index.json` (the app's: per route `file, caption, role, atM, v` = hash) and `routes/previews/renders.json` (the tool's)
- **Time:** ~1–2 min to load a park world (25–120 s), 15–90 s a picture on this shared 2-CPU machine; a full render ≈ 1.5–2.5 h of lock time in ~21 batches (one world each)
- **Offline:** the manifest is precached (sw-assets.js via build_routes); the pictures are not (they'd add ~1.3 MB to every app update) — sw.js keeps each the first time it's shown, cache-first in `bikefree-previews-v1`, which survives app updates; `?v=<hash>` makes a re-render a new entry and the page prunes entries no longer listed
- QC: `node --test test/route-previews.test.mjs` 11 of 11 (picker, subject skip, captions, every menu route planned 2–3 spots, manifest files / WebP / budget, no-pictures case, menu / SW wiring); `build_routes` + `--check` clean (190 offline files). Full `npm test` left to the merge. No menu screenshots taken (wrapped up early)
- State at wrap-up: 20 pictures (0.24 MB) for 11 routes — the parks Yosemite, Acadia, Teton, Crater Lake, Monument Valley (2), Big Sur, and Pittsburgh's Oakland / Phipps spots; the other 47 routes show no pictures yet; 6 of the 20 are from the first camera version (`--check` calls them stale)
- Follow-ups (F270–F279 kept for previews):
- [ ] F270 **Render the full set** once the scenes settle (6.1 clipping, 6.6 dressing): `flock /tmp/heavy.lock node tools/build_previews.mjs --stale --limit 1` in a loop until "rendering 0" (or one `node tools/build_previews.mjs` on a free machine), then `--check`; look through them (a contact sheet) and add hand picks where a spot is dull; then tighten the manifest test back to every route having ≥ 2 pictures
- [ ] F271 Menu screenshots (desktop 1440×900 and phone 390×844, light and dark) with a full set; check the phone strip's scroll and the enlarge dialog on a real phone
- [ ] F272 The subject check doesn't know what a ray hits: a picture passes if any of the subject's points is unblocked (a tower's top over the buildings counts); a stricter check (an ID render pass) would also catch a subject mostly hidden
- [ ] F273 Landforms are part of the terrain (heightAt is their surface): their check uses a point just over the top and their foot; a big rock wall close up (El Capitan) fills the frame but reads as "a hillside"
- [ ] F274 Starts are named after the start junction or the route ("Start · Yosemite Valley Loop"); parks could get real start names in picks.json

**6.5 Hill water** *(branch `sprint-dress`, from `pittsburgh-phase6-test` at 5946513, 19 Nov 2026)*
- **Data** (`tools/build_city.py`, F131): a water polygon more than `inlandWaterAboveM` (10 m) over the rivers is now a *pond* when it is flat (its interior's DEM samples within 3 m; a stream's within 1.2 m): land code 9 under it, and `ponds: [{name, way, kind, level, areaM2, ring}]` in the city data — `level` the median of the interior's ground (the DEM is flat on the water), for a fountain or pool 0.3 m under the lower quarter of its rim's ground (a basin); `kind` from the tags and name: reservoir, fountain, pool, pond. Water that falls along a hollow (Saw Mill Run's and Nine Mile Run's stream polygons) and polygons under 20 m² stay park; the build lists every one with why (`hillWater: false` turns it off). The build also reports streets running into a pond (none). Rebuilt: the city data of all nine areas (lines, terrain grids, network unchanged). Per area, water / park: core 14 / 5, Allegheny 14 / 5, West End 14 / 5, Oakland 14 / 5, Polish Hill 9 / 10, GAP & Homestead 20 / 5, North Side 7 / 4, East End 31 / 7, Slopes 11 / 5 — Highland Park's Reservoirs No. 1 and No. 2 (79,000 / 68,000 m²), Lake Carnegie, the entrance fountain and the zoo's pools; Panther Hollow Lake, Westinghouse Pond, the Mary Schenley fountain; Herron Hill Reservoir; Mellon Square's fountain downtown (a basin now, was a 12–16 m pit); the reservoirs on the Slopes' and Fineview's hills
- **Ground** (`city/landscape.js`): `pondAt(x, z, band)` (signed distance to the ring, on a 32 m grid of the ponds' boxes); under the water a basin (0.8 m under it at the edge, down to 2.8 m), round it a bank 0.35 m over the water for 1.6 m, easing down 1:2 to the hillside; kept through the route's bank (never over the route's road) and after the streets' grading (the basin under the water; the bank's top where no street runs within 3 m). `isWater` / `waterLevelAt` know the ponds. Streets and paths along a pond keep their carriageway and sidewalks 35 cm over its water (`liftOverPondsJob`, 8 % ramps, streets sharing a lifted node follow)
- **Drawing** (`city/city.js` `_pondsJob`): the toolbox's lake water (`makeLakeWaterMaterial`: shallows by the shore distance, ripples, sky and treeline reflection; dark at night like the rivers) on a grid of cells over each ring (0.75–4 m by size), cut at the ring in the shader; two draws (open water; fountain basins and pools, bluer) and one for the banks: a stone wall with a dark wet foot, a pale coping and an outer face down into the ground for reservoirs, basins and pools; a sloping earth bank with a wet band for ponds. Lamps, benches, trees and the kit's furniture keep off ponds (land code 9, `pondAt` with a 1.8 m margin)
- Test: `test/hill-water.test.mjs` (East End and Oakland: the named bodies at their own level over the rivers; ground under the water 0.5 m+ below it; the bank over it where no street or the route runs; no street within 0.2 m of a pond's water)

**6.4 Subway polish** *(branch `sprint-p6polish`, 19 Nov 2026)*
- **Trains (F125):** a stylised take on Pittsburgh's light-rail cars: each car two sections on a bellows articulation, cabs at both ends (coupled cab to cab), white with a red skirt and a black window band, lit (warm yellow) windows with riders' silhouettes, an amber destination sign, roof gear and a pantograph; one instance per section (8), so a train bends on the curves. Doors: two per side per section, their leaves slide open on the platform side 2.5 s after the train stops and close 3 s before it leaves (a vertex shader on the Builder's part ids; the opening behind is lit). Head-lights (warm white) on the lead cab and tail-lights (red) on the last, as camera-facing glows that carry through the tunnel air and the dark mouths (one instanced draw). The timetable is as it was (150 s, 18 s dwells; `trainState` now also says which leg and how far into it). Cost: 2 draws (trains, lights), under 1 k triangles a section
- **Sound:** the app had none, so a minimal mixer: `js/world/toolbox/core/sound.js` (`worldSound`: Web Audio, everything synthesised, no files; master level 0.35; made on first use, resumed on the first tap or key; fades out and suspends when nothing has played for 0.5 s — ride paused, tab hidden, area unloaded). Settings → **World sounds** (on by default, quiet; off = silent; `settings.worldSound`). The subway's ambience (`SubwaySound` in the kit): tunnel air below ground, the escalators' hum near them, a train's rumble by distance and speed, brakes as it stops, two door tones before it leaves, motors winding up as it pulls away, and a three-note station chime ~8 s before a train comes in at the platform you're near; faint at the headhouses, nothing on the street
- **North Side station (F121):** `tools/build_subway_line.py` now takes the T's tracks west to the real platform (OSM 233830502 / 557202013) and puts the North Side escalator's foot on it (lon −80.00966, the real platform −80.0092…−80.0102; ~125 m west-north-west of the old foot in Red Lot 5A). The way: NS, Mazeroski Way north, Reedsdale St west past the West General Robinson St Garage, left down Tony Dorsett Dr and left into the headhouse beside the garage's north side (clear of the I-279 ramps over the lots further west: a first try with the headhouse on the lot under them creased a ramp's ribbon), down eastward onto the platform (under the garage's north side, along Reedsdale), then the tunnel as before. Junction NS and its arms unchanged (straight on is still Mazeroski Way north, F120), keys and `keySplits` unchanged, saved routes and presets carry on. NS>GW 1.07 → 1.36 km (+24 / −24 m; escalators 329–377 and 1251–1299 m of the line, platforms 377–437 and 1191–1251, river 760–1030; cover over the tunnel ≥ 12.9 m); the subway area's line 3.34 → 3.63 km; the network 132.77 → 133.06 km; presets Under the Allegheny 4.6 → 4.9 km, Three Rivers Loop · via the subway 16.4 → 16.7 km, Long Loop · out by the subway 34.7 → 35.0 km. Rebuilt: the line, the area's city data (`syntheticWays` 310–1316 m; the parking aisle along the garage's north side dropped: it ran over the shaft), its terrain grid, the core's `subway.heads` (`bake_subway_heads.mjs`, `--check` clean), the network, routes and presets. - [x] 7.3 **Polka-dot clouds:** a dotted / speckled look on the clouds on every route — find the cause and fix it. Follow-ups F320–F324
  - Cause: the sky's cloud ring (js/world/stylized-terrain.js `_makeClouds`) drew every cloud with the same texture — one round five-puff blob (the cumulus atlas's four look-alike heaps on Alpe d'Huez and Going-to-the-Sun) — at about the same size on screen (the vista kit's width grows with distance), at golden-angle steps (2.399 rad, the most even spread there is) and in one height band: 24–34 equal round dots evenly sprinkled round the horizon. Headless (swiftshader, dpr 1 and 2) the cloud pixels themselves are clean (no dither, no alpha-to-coverage, no z-fight: the clouds don't write depth, have no alpha test and no post pass touches them), so nothing GPU-only was found in the cloud path
  - Fix: clouds gather in groups of 1–4 overlapping billboards at random bearings with open sky between, sizes vary a lot (×0.55–1.85 per group, smaller members), heights jitter within a group; outside the vista kit a new soft atlas (`makeSoftCloudAtlas`, vista.js: four long, uneven heaps of 8–12 flattened puffs) replaces the round blob, which stays only for the peak caps. Same 26 / 24–34 quads, one draw call: no cost. `tools/snap_scene.mjs --dpr 2` added (Retina snapshots)
  - Check on the bike: clouds read as a few bigger, flatter, uneven formations with clear gaps, not a row of same-size round dots; if you still see a fine dotted *pixel* pattern on or near clouds, that's another cause — say where (sky or a far hillside) and the time of day
- [x] 7.3b **Fine pixel speckle** (user, 19 Nov: what they saw was a *fine pixel speckle*, not the cloud shapes 7.3 fixed): the prime suspects from 7.3's review (F320) — the far-terrain hand-off's screen-door dither (js/world/toolbox/materials/surfaces.js `bfFarH`, interleaved-gradient noise + `discard`, a halftone of dots on hills ~1.5 km off, the sky and clouds showing through it) and alpha-to-coverage on the far tree cards (js/world/toolbox/vegetation/tree-cards.js `alphaToCoverage: true`, which Apple GPUs dither); also check any other screen-door / alphaTest paths. Replace the screen-door hand-off with a smooth one (e.g. an overlap crossfade in colour toward the far mesh/fog, or depth-sorted blending) and drop alpha-to-coverage where it speckles (plain alphaTest + mipmapped alpha, or a soft edge), keeping the look; Follow-ups F325-range taken: use F320–F324 (7.3's)
  - Cause: the far-terrain hand-off (the ring ~1.3–1.7 km out where the stylized ground meets the real far terrain) was a screen-door dither: js/world/toolbox/materials/surfaces.js discarded the stylized ground's pixels by an interleaved-gradient noise of `gl_FragCoord` (`bfFarH`), so across the band each pixel showed either the stylized or the real ground — a fixed halftone of dots on every far hillside (sky and clouds through it at ridges), finer at Retina dpr 2. Confirmed headless: /tmp/landmark-shots/speckle-before.jpg (Yosemite preview spot, dpr 2, `--far`, zoomed). Second cause (GPU-only, reasoned from code): alpha-to-coverage on the far tree cards (js/world/toolbox/vegetation/tree-cards.js) — far cards are a few pixels tall, all edge, and Apple GPUs resolve partial coverage as a dither pattern. No other screen-door / `gl_FragCoord` noise path in js (the other discards are hard cut-outs; post.js has no grain or dither)
  - Fix: the ground fades out across the band instead (`bfFarA = 1 − bfFarT`, blended; `setGroundHandoff` switches the ground material to alpha blending only while the far terrain is on, alpha kept ≥ the destination's so the canvas stays opaque); the far terrain's tiles draw first (`renderOrder −0.5`: after the sky, before the ground) and stay solid under the whole band, so the band is a colour crossfade with no gaps or z-fight. Tree cards: plain alpha test (no alpha-to-coverage), mip-blurred alpha scaled up by mip level (`× 1 + 0.2·lod`) so far cards keep their size. After: /tmp/landmark-shots/speckle-after.jpg — the band is smooth. `tools/snap_scene.mjs --far` (wait for the real far horizon) added. Tests: scenery.test.mjs
  - Check on the bike: hillsides ~1.5 km off read as smooth ground fading into the real terrain, no dotted band; the far forest edge reads clean, not fizzing. If a speckle remains, say where (sky, a hillside, the forest) and the time of day
  - Follow-ups: F320 — measure frame rate on the Air with the far terrain on (the ground draws blended while it is; one shader compile when the far horizon switches on); F321 — faint 1-px dotted seam lines between ground patches on far hillsides (in the before shot too: cracks along patch edges, not the hand-off); F322 — far tree cards now alias at their edges without alpha-to-coverage (watch for crawl on the Air)

Into the turn (+ right, − left), capped at 38°, 0 when stopped, eased first-order with τ 0.4 s (63 % in 0.4 s, ~95 % in 1.2 s; ≤ 1.6° a frame at 60 fps in every ride probed)
  - **Numbers:** 25 km/h on a 10 m radius → **26.2°**, on a 30 m radius → **9.3°**; 40 km/h on 30 m → 22.8°; the cap from r < 6.3 m at 25 km/h. Rides (60 fps, eased): East Carson & 10th's right turn (8 m curve) peaks +9° at 12 km/h, +33° at 25 km/h, +37° at 40 km/h; the Water Steps' U-turn hits the −38° cap (left, from the right lane) even at 13 km/h (its curve swings ~3.5 m across the road: a radius of ~2 m); Bakery Square's hairpin at 25 km/h −37°; Alpe d'Huez's hairpins at 12 km/h climbing ~11–13°, at 40 km/h (descending) the 38° cap; a plain route's end U-turn at 20 km/h −37° for ~1 s, upright 15 m later; the Pittsburgh short loop at 25 km/h averages 1.4° (straight streets: ~0)
  - **The model** (js/world/rider.js `setLean(rad)` / `lean`): the whole skinned mesh (bike, rider, the pedalling and posture animation, which run as before inside it) rolls about the model's ground line along its length — the tyres' contact line — so the wheels stay on the road; the soft shadow stays flat. world.js eases `_lean` each frame from `_leanTarget(s, lane, speed, path)` and sets it after `rider.update`; the camera follows the rider's position (unchanged), so it stays level. The stand-in before the model loads is left upright

- [x] 7.8b **Stadiums memory** (user bug, 20 Nov: on a phone the Stadiums Loop crashes mid-load — the browser kills the page and reloads it, landing on the menu with the "unsaved ride" prompt; Schenley Park loads fine). Cause: memory — the Stadiums Loop keeps three areas resident (core, North Side, subway) and the full stadium kit (stands, decks, ~2,000–2,600 fans each, players) is built into all 7 areas' scenes that can see the stadiums; headless heap 1.1–1.7 GB on that ride. Fix (user: cut memory now, helps desktop too): build each stadium's full kit (ridden deck, escalator, crowd, players) only in the area(s) whose line rides through it; elsewhere a light far shell (bowl silhouette, lights, no crowd/players/decks); share geometry/materials across stadiums; smaller, instanced crowds with a near/far LOD; check other heavy per-area duplicates (landmark heroes copied into many areas). Target: the Stadiums Loop's peak heap clearly below the pre-stadium baseline + a small margin. Follow-ups F359–F369 range
  - **Measured cause** (headless, tools: the stadium-ride probe with an unlimited area budget, `gc()` before each reading; per-area geometry by mesh name): the stadium kit was ~49 MB of geometry (typed arrays, and as much again on the GPU) across the three resident areas — core PNC 8.3 + Acrisure 12.1, North Side 8.3 + 4.9, subway 10.9 + 4.9 MB — most of it the deck's railings (two beams a side every 0.5 m), with ~90–110 MB of short-lived build garbage per area. `usedJSHeapSize` doesn't count ArrayBuffers, so the JS heap barely sees it. **The ride's real peak is something else:** at 9.6–12 km of the loop (round the Point) the F220 rule (`areasReachable`: areas a turn at a junction within 800 m could reach) also builds **Allegheny and Polish Hill**, so five areas are resident: +300 MB of live heap (1.15 → 1.45 GB after GC). The pre-stadium tree (c933d5c, same path) builds the same two, but for a shorter stretch (the detours moved the metres): peak 1.33 GB after GC
  - **Fix** (js/world/toolbox/structures/stadium.js): a stadium is built in full (ridden deck, escalator, ramp colonnade, crowd, players) only in an area whose own `routeDecks` name it (`riddenStadiums`: the core both, the North Side's and the subway's aprons PNC Park — they carry its deck, and the rider can be drawn in them on it); elsewhere a **far shell**: 96 rays (240 in full), 4 bands of one slope each (no treads), the outer wall, the field's grass / infield / end zones, boards, light towers and their night glows, the helices' drums, a plain slab every ~4 m where the deck runs; no crowd, players, railings, escalator, arches or colonnade. Shell geometry is cached at module level by (stadium, field level) and shared by the areas resident together (refcounted, released with the last); materials stay per area (a world's light bake patches them). Full kit: the deck's railings / balustrades / posts every 2 m (the top, the ridden surface, still every 0.5 m), ~27 % fewer fans, and a near/far LOD — the near-concourse rows and the players shown (and the players ticked) only within 300 m of the stands, the far crowd within 1.5 km
  - **After** (same probe, 0–24 km, two laps): stadium geometry in the three areas 49.4 → **22.7 MB** (core 20.4 → 11.4, North Side 13.2 → 5.1, subway 15.8 → 6.2; a shell 0.3 MB); all geometry 355 → 343 MB (pre-stadium 318); renderer geometries at 24 km 957 → **890** (pre-stadium ~833), textures 60 → 59 (59); JS heap after GC at load 1134 → 1113 MB (pre 1068), peak after GC 1479 → 1456 (pre 1328), peak raw 1918 → 1841 (pre 1361). So the geometry/GPU cost is halved, but the JS-heap peak isn't near the baseline: that peak is the two off-plan areas (F359). PNC's upper-deck view 315 → 311 draws, 2.24 → 1.98 M triangles; snapshot /tmp/landmark-shots/pnc-upper-deck-after-mem.jpg (crowd, railings, field and skyline as before)

An off-route turn into a cold area may show 6.2's short "Loading the streets ahead…" instead of the area already being there; never a blank screen (the drawn area stays while it covers the rider). - [x] 7.11 **Main page route list loads slowly** (user bug, 20 Nov: the route list takes over 15 s to appear, on the Mac and the phone, every time — reloads too) *(branch `sprint7-load`)*
  - **Measured** (`node tools/menu_load.mjs`: headless Chrome, a local server serving like GitHub Pages — gzip, max-age 600, ETag; cold = fresh profile, warm = reloaded with the service worker installed): first card **6.1 s** cold / **6.7 s** warm unthrottled, **7.7 s / 7.0 s** at 10 Mbps · 40 ms; **650 requests, 13.6 MB** (gzipped) before the list. Same at `cecc3dc` (7.6 s) and `5954750` (#38, the city of areas: 7.6 s); 1.5 s before it (`83110de`) — it regressed with the Pittsburgh network and has grown with every area
  - **Causes** (js/app.js `refreshRouteList`): (1) the list waited for **every route to load**, and each route load also fetched its **scenery** (city data, terrain grids, places: ~40 MB raw over 31 scenes); (2) a **race**: the city's ~48 routes loaded at once and each, finding no network yet, fetched and parsed the city loop + its scenery and all 10 areas' GPX again and built the network again (428 requests to routes/cities, 158 to routes/scenes); (3) the area GPX were fetched one after another; (4) the preview manifest was awaited before the catalog; (5) the service worker registered at boot and its install precaches the whole app (195 files, ~47 MB) while the menu loads. The service worker is network-first, so a warm start was no faster
  - **Fix**: the list renders from the catalog at once — tools/build_routes.mjs bakes each listed route's card numbers (`stats`: the same fields the card reads from the loaded route) and a ≤121-point thumbnail `outline` into routes/parks/index.json (+31 kB, 12 kB gzipped); no GPX or scenery before the list. Scenery loads when a ride starts (`openWorld`), not with the route. Route and network loads in flight are shared (`routeLoads`, `netBuilds`: each file fetched and the network built once); area GPX fetched together; manifest, catalog, ideas and your library in parallel; your own routes and the city's routes load after the list shows (then the cards and an open city panel refresh); the service worker registers once those are in and the page is idle. Card text, tags, the city panel and rides unchanged (card text diffed against 7c7cf36: identical)
  - **After** (same tool): first card **0.37 s** cold / **0.43 s** warm unthrottled, **0.74 s / 0.41 s** at 10 Mbps; every thumbnail drawn (the Pittsburgh map included) by **0.7–1.2 s**; **42 requests, 207 kB** before the list, 64 requests / ~500 kB in all
  - Checks: `node tools/build_routes.mjs` + `--check`; test/city-routes, offline, route-previews, gpx 58/58; headless smoke: menu, city panel (48 routes, facts), Yosemite ride and a Pittsburgh city ride open the 3D world with scenery, no errors
  - Follow-ups (F390–F394 kept for 7.11):
  - [ ] F390 The Pittsburgh card's map thumbnail still needs the whole network (city loop + 10 areas' GPX, ~250 kB gzipped, ~0.3 s CPU) and 11 scene files for rivers; a baked card outline (network lines + rivers) in pittsburgh-map.json would draw it with the list
  - [ ] F391 The service worker is network-first for every file: each start revalidates everything, and each new build precaches ~47 MB (all scenery). Cache-first (stale-while-revalidate) for data under routes/ keyed by the app revision, and precaching only the app code + catalog (scenery cached on first ride or by the offline download), would make warm starts and new builds lighter
  - [ ] F392 The selected route's preview pane waits on its GPX (a Pittsburgh preset: the city network); it could show the catalog's numbers and outline first
  - [ ] F393 Measured headless against a local server only: check on the Mac and the phone (raw.githack / GitHub Pages) that the list now appears in about a second
  - [ ] F394 tools/menu_load.mjs throttles the page through CDP, which doesn't throttle the service worker's own fetches, so warm throttled numbers are optimistic; the cold throttled "bytes before the list" also undercounts

### Phase 8: load before you ride *(test branch `phase8-load-test`, from `main` after PR #40, 20 Nov 2026; one PR at the end)*
The user: a ride starts at once (time and distance record) while its scenery is still loading. Wanted: menu and route choice → load the route's assets behind a loading screen → then the ride begins.
Before: Ride hid the menu and showed the ride screen at once; only the start area's scene file was fetched first; terrain tiles, roads, trees, buildings, baked light, rider / pacer models and the next areas built in frame-budget slices on the ride screen (a thin `#hLoading` bar); `session.autoStart` was armed at once, so the first pedal stroke (or a resume) started the clock.
Work packages A (world readiness), B (loading screen + clock), C (checks), done without subagents.
- [x] L1 Loading screen (`#loadScreen`, over the ride screen; fades in after 0.15 s so a world that's ready at once never flashes it): route name, distance / climb / time from the ride's plan (else the lap), the route's first preview picture, a progress bar with what's loading and a chip per step (Scenery, Ground, Roads & buildings, Light, Riders). Cancel goes back to the menu (a press of A / Space meanwhile doesn't start anything there)
- [x] L2 `World.readiness()` → `{ ready, failed, progress, steps }`. Ready = the scenery's assets in, the ground tiles round the start built, the city chunks within 450 m built (`CityScene.pendingNear`; on a city of areas also an area the path enters within 500 m), the first bake done, the rider and pacer models loaded. `World.setPreload(true)` while the screen is up: ground tiles and city chunks build at 40 ms a frame, the start's areas at 60 ms, and the view is drawn only every 4th frame (its programs still compile behind the screen). Later Pittsburgh areas load during the ride as before
  - The real horizon is waited for too when its tiles are in flight: it turning on re-makes the stylized ground (2.5 s of main thread on the headless box), which used to land on the first pedal strokes. Offline it never arrives and isn't waited for
- [x] L3 Clock held: `session.autoStart` off and `session.setHeld(true)` behind the screen (no auto start, no resume); a paused ride resumes only once ready. The trainer stays connected, its resistance the start's grade (the rider doesn't move)
- [x] L4 Then "Pedal to start" (the HUD's own pill): time and distance begin on the first pedal stroke after the screen closes
- [x] L5 Mid-ride: while the world waits for an area (`World.waitingForArea`: the fade, "Loading the streets ahead…") the ride is held (`Session.setHeld`: as a pause, so the .fit has the gap; the speed you ride at still shows), and carries on by itself when the area is ready — unless you paused meanwhile. Past 30 s the clock runs on (toast) until that wait ends
- [x] L6 Safety valve: a scenery file failing, or 30 s without ready, shows "Ride anyway" (today's behaviour); loading carries on and the screen still closes by itself if it gets there first
- [x] L7 Checks: `test/load-before-ride.test.mjs` (the hold: no start before ready, first pedal stroke after, A while loading, mid-ride hold, pause during a hold, auto-pause); `node tools/load_screen_check.mjs [--hold] [--cancel] [--phone] [--draw]` drives the app in headless Chrome (Demo on, Start) and fails if the clock moves while the screen is up. Tests 312/312
  - Headless here (software GPU, view not drawn): Route 28 screen 12 s (5 s without the real-horizon wait), Three Rivers Loop 39 s, Stadiums Loop 47 s; Pittsburgh's last step is the first bake (Light), which runs on the GPU, so these are far from the Mac's. Clock started ≤ 0.2 s after the screen closed every time; mid-ride hold held and resumed on all three; Cancel answered in 75–220 ms; phone-sized window (844×390) the same
- [x] L9 Phone crash (user, 20 Nov: most of the way through loading a Pittsburgh route the phone reloaded the page — "an unsaved ride…"). Measured headless, phone-sized, Stadiums Loop, live JS heap (forced GC): main ~1.0–1.1 GB, this branch ~1.1–1.15 GB; the loading screen's faster build isn't what drives it (no boost: the same peak; the plan's other areas off: the same). Two device-only pressures fixed here:
  - F391 (from the follow-up list): the service worker precached **47 MB** (every route's scenes, terrain, city files, the rider) on the first visit of every new build — each githack test link is a new build — at the same time as the ride loaded. Now it precaches only the app (122 files, 3.4 MB); route data and models are cached the first time a ride uses them, cache-first in the revision's cache (`sw.js`, `tools/build_routes.mjs`). Network-first stays for the app's code. Trade-off: offline, only routes ridden once online are there
  - (the same change is the main page's "persistently slow" load on real devices: every build re-downloaded 47 MB in the background while the menu loaded, and network-first re-fetched and re-stored every big scene file on every ride)
- [x] F399 Pittsburgh's memory at the start (user, 20 Nov: still crashed on the phone with the SW fix). `node tools/memory_check.mjs "<preset>"` (headless, phone-sized, after GC, every mesh uploaded once). What held it (Three Rivers Loop, main): ~4.7 M triangles for the core area, built whole; ~320 MB of geometry arrays kept on the CPU after upload (buildings ~170 MB: ~2.5 M kit vertices at 72 B), the same again on the GPU (shared memory on a phone); ~210 MB of build data kept (the street grid ~60 MB, junction plans ~40 MB, kit / street-kit plans). Done:
  - Static city meshes' CPU copy released once uploaded (`js/world/toolbox/core/release.js`; the app turns it on, tools leave it off; `?keepGeometry=1` keeps it): sector buildings, the building kit's sectors, streets / sidewalks / paths, the street kit's chunks. Not bridges (the river life reads their piers), not the ground (pooled)
  - Sectors whose chunk list never emptied (a chunk with only street-kit content) were never merged: they're now merged once every chunk is built (city and building kit) — also fewer draw calls (1131 → 743 meshes)
  - The street grid packed (numeric cells, one number per segment)
  - Result: Three Rivers Loop live JS heap 632 → 430 MB, geometry on the CPU 320 → 143 MB; Stadiums Loop 781 → 556 MB (174 MB). Screenshots identical with and without the release; tests 312/312
  - Not done: the building attributes' precision (with the CPU copy gone it would save only ~35 MB on the GPU, and the windows' layout needs u, v and the bay widths exact); releasing the junction plans / kit plans (city life and the junctions read them while riding)
- [ ] F400 Next if the phone still crashes: build buildings and street detail by distance (~1.5 km) instead of the whole area (the bake needs only up-sun occluders within 2.5 km); a phone mode (one area at a time; less near detail); measure the GPU side on the device (Safari's Web Inspector → Timelines → Memory)
- [ ] L8 Ride check on the Mac and the phone (raw.githack): how long the screen is up on a park route, a Pittsburgh preset and the Stadiums Loop; that the first pedal stroke starts the clock; Cancel; a mid-ride wait into an area off the plan
- Open: F396 the screen's timings on real devices may still want a shorter wait (e.g. not waiting for the real horizon on phones); F397 the old "Preparing scenery…" bake overlay still exists under the screen (now only seen after Ride anyway); F398 the loading screen has no map outline when a route has no preview picture (80 missing, F270)

### PGH: a free-ride map of every street *(test branch `pgh-test`, from `main` after PR #41, 21 Nov 2026; one PR at the end)*
The user: a new map "PGH" for bikefree — no buildings; the road network placed and every road and connection usable by the cyclist; no traffic, pedestrians or routes; free ride only. The road data was to come from a Cities: Skylines II save ("Pittsburgh Build 3-2025"); that save only downloads to a signed-in game client and is compressed game data, so PGH is built from OpenStreetMap (what a 1:1 CS2 build is traced from), fetched here from the OSM API. Chose: the **current network corridor** (900 m round the existing Pittsburgh areas' lines) and **streets + trails, no highways**.
- [x] `tools/build_pgh.py` (config `tools/city/pgh.json`): OSM tiles → the rideable ways (streets, alleys, cycleways, trails, bike-allowed paths, crosswalks; no motorways, no sidewalks, no steps, nothing `bicycle=no` or private) → a junction graph: split at shared nodes, junctions under 15 m apart merged (dual carriageways, offset crossings), parallel duplicates dropped, bends joined away (a junction is a real choice), spurs under 30 m pruned, the largest connected piece kept. Trunk boulevards (Saw Mill Run Blvd…) only as pieces of ≤ 180 m between streets — crossing them, never riding along them (without this the South Hills, ~80 km of streets, were cut off). Heights: AWS terrain tiles, bridge decks interpolated between their ground ends, smoothed, ends pinned to one height per junction, ≤ 30 % over 10 m (an edge steeper on average than 34 % is dropped). → `routes/cities/pgh.json` (9,812 junctions, 14,428 edges, 1,656 km; 4.4 MB, ~0.75 MB gzipped) + `routes/cities/pgh-ground.grid.bin.gz` (20 m ground)
- [x] `js/graph.js`: the graph as a ride network — every edge laid end to end on a synthetic, mirrored base line (with 10 m pads between edges), so NetRide, the turn prompt (◀ ▶ / A D), U-turns, the minimap and the path route ride it unchanged
- [x] `js/world/graph-world.js`: a light world for a graph — every street, trail and bridge streamed in 400 m tiles round the camera (roads, centre lines, junction pads, embankment skirts, bridge decks with parapets), the ground cut down beside every road and under every bridge, one river plane; the rider and chase camera; no buildings, traffic or people. ~100 draw calls, ~0.2 M triangles
- [x] The app: "PGH · Free ride" in the route list (its data loads only when picked); the world switches to the graph world for it
- [x] `tools/qc_pgh.mjs` (`npm run qc:pgh`) + `test/pgh.test.mjs`: one connected piece, every way through every junction (95,752), edge ends on their junctions (no gaps or steps), no jumps along an edge, grades, 200 simulated random-turn rides (5,000 km) without a jump — **0 fails**; warnings: 61 short pitches over 32 %, 45 places where two ways cross at nearly the same height without a junction (grade separations whose decks interpolate low)
- [x] `tools/snap_pgh.mjs` + `tools/pgh-preview.html`: headless snapshots anywhere (`name@lat,lon[,dir][@m]`, `--view back,up` for an overview)
- [x] Review (`python3 tools/review_pgh.py` → `node tools/review_pgh_map.mjs`, map artifact "PGH road review"): dead ends, pieces not connected, dense junction clusters, river and bridge heights. User (21 Nov): dead ends — flag only, don't change yet; disconnected pieces — stay out, never added back; simplify + alleys — remove; bridges, riverside paths, overpasses — fix as proposed
- [x] Cleanup in the build (`finish()` in tools/build_pgh.py): removed 119 turn slips/links, 176 crosswalk/trail bits, 204 short ways in dense clusters, 7 trails on a bridge beside its road and 1,025 alleys (134 km) — each only where both junctions keep ≥ 2 other ways and the network stays in one piece; junctions left with two ways joined away. River bridges (≥ 40 m over water): deck ends from the bank top (20 m look-round), ≥ `bridgeClearM` over the river ramping down at `bridgeRampPct`, approaches eased at ≤ 6 % for up to 120 m, deck-to-street corners rounded. Riverside ways (≤ 25 m from the river) ≥ 2.5 m above the pool, smoothed twice as long. Near-level crossings cleared to ≥ 4.5 m by dipping the way under (8–16 % easings), lifting the deck, or removing the minor way. → 7,579 junctions, 10,663 edges, 1,502 km. Review: river/bridge problems 152 → 18 (low decks 21 → 5, all within ~1 m but one; deck kinks 56 → 9; on the river 30 → 1; crossings 45 → 1); dense clusters 328 → 95 (nothing minor left to cut in them); QC 0 fails
- [x] Overlapping roads (user, 21 Nov): ways drawn on top of each other (centre lines closer than half their widths, within 3 m in height, ≥ 20 m, away from junctions) — 174 pairs, mostly both directions of divided roads (Glenwood Bridge, Banksville Rd, Birmingham Bridge, Negley Run Blvd…) and trails beside streets. `remove_overlaps()` takes out the minor way of each pair (else the other) where no junction is left with < 2 ways: 144 ways (37 km) removed, 5 overlaps left that can't go. → 4,199 junctions, 7,039 edges, 1,117 km; 0 dead ends; QC 0 fails
- [x] Similar roads close by + smoothing (user, 21 Nov: simplify first, then smooth). `remove_parallels()`: ways side by side (headings within 20°) ≤ 30 m apart for ≥ 60 m — the minor way of each pair goes (never a dead end or a split): 108 ways (54 km). `smooth_lines()`: 8 passes of a 1-2-1 average on each way's inner points, each kept within 8 m of its mapped place (zigzags 162 → 0 roads, sharp corners 646 → 201 — mostly hairpins, bends into junctions 358 → 89). `smooth_heights()`: each junction a little sloped plate fitted to the first ~30 m of its ways; ways leave it at the plate's grade, ease into their own profile, the break spread over the first ~50 m (grade jumps > 15 % a step: 2,370 → 16 roads). Overlaps, similar roads and crossings checked again after smoothing. → 4,058 junctions, 6,786 edges, 1,063 km; QC 0 fails, 0 warnings
- [x] Pittsburgh routes on PGH (user, 21 Nov: "map them to our map, try not to create roads, only the route details"): `node tools/map_pgh_routes.mjs --lines` writes the 48 routes' lines (`tools/city/pgh-route-lines.json`); the build keeps every way they ride through the cleanup (a stretch of a sidewalk path, plaza or boulevard only where no street beside it carries the route; a removal between two ways near a route always takes the one farther from it) and adds 10 short links (278 m) where a route stepped between ways PGH didn't join. `node tools/map_pgh_routes.mjs --write` matches each route onto PGH (HMM over positions on its ways) → `presets` in pgh.json + `routes/cities/pgh-presets.json` (the app's list); `npm run pgh:build` runs both. All 48 close with no breaks; 31 follow the old line ≥ 90 % (lowest 67 %: the subway routes — the subway isn't rideable — and a few authored stretches with no OSM way: the West End Bridge approach, the Point, Hays). In the app as "PGH · …" routes (Rough Drafts) on the same network as the free ride
- [x] PGH landmarks — locations only (`python3 tools/pgh_landmarks.py` → `routes/cities/pgh-landmarks.json`, 82 places, 54 with an OSM outline). Road clashes resolved by judgement (user, 21 Nov): small monuments and buildings moved to the nearest spot clear of roads (Point of View 8 m, Doughboy 18 m, Vietnam Veterans Memorial 8 m, St Stanislaus 14 m, Waterfront stacks 42 m); Mon Incline spans McArdle Roadway; Phipps and PNC Park keep the paths in their grounds; Duquesne Incline's upper station merged into the incline
- [ ] F-PGH1: the 18 river/bridge findings left (Saw Mill Run Blvd 30 % kink at the West End, Simonton St, the Washington's Landing back-channel deck at 5 m, Bridle Trail over Wilmot Rd)
- [x] Dead ends (user, 21 Nov): bridges are fine for now (the review's bridge rules are off); every dead-end branch removed (2,156 ways, 311 km) except bridges; dead-end bridges connected through the nearest mapped way a bike could use (≤ 350 m, ≤ 12 % average, sidewalks/footways allowed, no motorways or steps), the connector leaving at the deck's own grade and easing in (joins: ≤ 8 % grade change but one, 62nd Street Bridge 18 %); pieces not in the network removed from the review and map too — 28 connected; the 8 that could not be (Wabash Tunnel, 40th Street Bridge, North Shore Trail, Glass Run Road, Ginkgo Trail, 2 × Saw Mill Run Blvd, 1 unnamed) removed with their branches (21 ways, 10 km). → 4,353 junctions, 7,337 edges, 1,154 km; 0 dead ends; QC 0 fails
- [ ] F-PGH2: start places (choose where the free ride starts: Point State Park, Hot Metal Bridge, Schenley Park…) instead of the first junctions along the way
- [ ] F-PGH3: a card thumbnail for PGH (the network's outline); a pacer on the graph world
- [ ] F-PGH4: grow the corridor (the whole city) once phones handle 1,656 km comfortably; streets cut at the corridor's edge end in a U-turn (1,777 dead ends, most of them cul-de-sacs and trail ends)

## Running it

Web Bluetooth needs Chrome and either `localhost` or HTTPS.

```sh
cd bikefree
python3 -m http.server 8000
# open http://localhost:8000 in Chrome
```

Close Zwift, Zwift Companion and the Wahoo app before connecting — they can hold the Bluetooth connections.

### hud-test — Minimap and power bar size *(test branch `hud-test`, on top of v0.12.1)*
- [x] Ride-screen minimap (bottom-left): route outline, this leg's progress, rider arrow, pacer dot; on routes and scenery loops
- [x] Settings → Minimap: Whole course (default) · Zoomed in, follows you · Off; <kbd>M</kbd> cycles it on the ride screen
- [x] Settings → Power bar size: Small (default, ~50% of the old size) · Medium · Large (original); labels keep a readable minimum

### ride-feel-test — riders' feel notes *(branch `ride-feel-test`)*
From the group ride: screen sleeps, a 1% hill "felt like a truck", shifts feel small, bike too far away.
- [x] Keep the screen awake while a trainer is connected or a ride is going (Screen Wake Lock, `js/wakelock.js`)
- [x] Power check: watts come straight from the KICKR; physics (CdA 0.32, Crr 0.004, profile weight + bike) gives real-world numbers — accurate, but full-strength hills with no momentum felt harsh in ERG (gear 12 @ 85 rpm: 0→1% was 98→163 W at once)
- [x] Hill feel setting 25/50/75/100%, default 50% like Zwift's trainer difficulty — scales the grade sent to the trainer only (flat and on-screen speed unchanged)
- [x] Momentum: the trainer's grade eases toward the road's over ~3 s (time constant) instead of jumping; shifts still act at once (replaced in 6.11 by real momentum, the felt grade's shaping and a descent floor)
- [x] Camera setting: Close (default, 3.3 m back and lower, ~1.3× bigger rider, FOV widens with speed, slight pull-back on acceleration, faint road buzz) or Classic (old fixed 4.5 m)
- [x] Diagnostics show "Trainer feels X% (road Y%, hill feel Z%)"
- [ ] Gear spacing: no change for now (revisit after riders try Hill feel + momentum)
